#2145. What next for Blackburn Rovers as Tony Mowbray steps back? New boss stance, impact on players and more

It marks the second time Mowbray has done so, temporarily withdrawing from his position at Birmingham City in February 2024 after being diagnosed with bowel cancer.

The news has been met with an outpouring of support from across English football, and deservedly so. Mowbray is one of the most universally liked figures in our game, and all hope to see him recover speedily.

Blackburn Rovers fans have sent their best wishes and got behind Mowbray first, of course. However, the news has understandably led to questions over what happens next at Ewood Park.

At the time of writing, it appears Blackburn Rovers will not be appointing a new head coach – not permanently, anyway. The wording of the club’s statement confirming Mowbray’s situation said he is ‘stepping back’, not stepping down, leaving, or anything similar.

First-team coaches Damien Johnson and Mike Dodds are taking over duties for the time being, and no further statement will be made, the club says.

The situation played out similarly upon Mowbray’s withdrawal from duties at Birmingham City. His long-term assistant Mark Venus took charge in his absence before the boss took formal medical leave and Gary Rowett was appointed on an interim basis.

This is a new and developing situation, but Rovers’ statement indicates a new boss will not be coming in at this moment in time. Trusted local reporter Elliott Jackson has since stated on X that the club ‘are certainly not looking for a successor right now’.

Without arguing semantics but to add clarity, I’m told Tony Mowbray has not ‘resigned’ or ‘stepped down’.He’s ‘stepped back’ to take a period to focus on his health. How long is that for? We don’t know.Club are certainly not looking for a successor right now. #Rovers🌹 https://t.co/9M8aWSEgsx

Mowbray cuts a hugely popular figure at Ewood Park. After relegation in 2016/17, he led them back from League One and consolidated their place back in the Championship before moving on at the end of his contract in 2022.

There were some reservations over his return though. It felt like an easy and crowd-pleasing move, but some questioned whether Mowbray could replicate his previous success after his absence and ill-fated second stint as West Brom boss.

However, Mowbray quickly made his presence felt at Blackburn Rovers, and they have overachieved in the first 15 games of the campaign. A side widely touted as relegation candidates now sits 13th in the table, three points off the play-offs and with big wins over top sides like West Ham, Millwall and Middlesbrough.

Mowbray has brought forward-thinking back to Ewood Park. It has seen creative aces like Todd Cantwell and Dapo Afolayan thrive, and star striker Andri Gudjohnsen has benefitted massively. Furthermore, the boss’ track record with developing youngsters has helped Kristi Montgomery come on leaps and bounds.

The 62-year-old has given Rovers fans something to celebrate again after tough years with poor football and growing frustration towards the ownership. Of course, hostility towards the hierarchy has not waned, but the quality of the football on display and positivity surrounding Mowbray’s return have made for a welcome change.

This is an unforeseen development that Blackburn Rovers have no choice but to deal with. Losing Mowbray, even if only temporarily, is a big blow, and it will impact the players.

Johnson and Dodds will focus on offering continuity. It will be smart to stick to Mowbray’s methods and a similar tactical plan, but they must be prepared to make bold decisions and necessary changes if form does falter.

Then, there’s the responsibility of the club hierarchy. It appears an external successor is not going to be named, but if Mowbray can not return to the dugout, the likes of Balaji Rao, Steve Curwood and Rudy Gestede have to be prepared with a plan to ensure it doesn’t derail their season.

When Birmingham City were met with the same situation, their initial action to promote Venus as interim boss did not work. Then, they acted too late to bring in an external replacement, and Rowett could not save them from relegation.

It is an awful predicament for Rovers, but they must be prepared for all scenarios to protect the club.

In the short term, though, Johnson, Dodds, the rest of the coaching staff and the Blackburn Rovers squad will be determined to deliver a derby day win over rivals Burnley for Mowbray as he recovers.

#2143. Barnsley developments hand Sheffield United & Derby County clear Reyes Cleary boost

Barnsley, Reyes Cleary, Sheffield United, and Derby County could all become central to an interesting January transfer situation, with the talented winger attracting attention from higher up the football pyramid.

Reyes Cleary has emerged as one of the most exciting attacking players in League One with Barnsley, and it is hardly surprising that Championship clubs are monitoring his situation again after having interest in the 22-year-old in the summer.

Both Sheffield United and Derby County have reason to strengthen their attacking options, while Barnsley face an increasingly important period in their own campaign. There could be some significant developments at Oakwell over the coming weeks ahead of January.

Reyes Cleary is arguably one of the most exciting and explosive wide players in League One, so it is little surprise that the likes of Sheffield United, Derby, and Bristol City have been credited with an interest in securing his services.

It's evident that he has the individual quality to make the step up to the Championship and would represent an exciting addition for any of the sides keen on him at a higher level.

However, Barnsley's latest defeat to Wigan Athletic has made their league position even more precarious, with the threat of relegation to League Two becoming increasingly difficult to ignore.

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

That could have significant implications for Cleary's future. Barnsley adopted a hardball stance during the summer and priced interested clubs out of a move, but their current predicament makes that approach considerably harder to justify.

Cleary might be vital to their survival hopes, but they cannot necessarily expect to retain him on their terms if Championship interest intensifies in January. Barnsley are now second-bottom and three points off safety, albeit with games in hand on some of the sides above them.

Barnsley deserve considerable credit for identifying Cleary's potential in the first place. He had shown promise during his time with Hartlepool United in the National League, but not many League One clubs would have taken the gamble of recruiting somebody two divisions higher and backing him to make the step up.

It has proved to be an inspired decision, although Barnsley now face a difficult situation with just 18 months remaining on his contract come January. Sheffield United and Derby are unlikely to be the only Championship clubs interested, either, with Bristol City also keen.

West Bromwich Albion should also have serious regrets about allowing Cleary to leave as early as they did. He looks every bit a player capable of establishing himself in the Championship, and a return to The Hawthorns could even be something worth exploring if they recognise their previous mistake.

That said, Barnsley have to be braced for offers and interest again, with it becoming increasingly to stand in the player's way if he is determined to test himself in the second tier. That said, Barnsley will be desperate to keep hold of arguably their best player.

There is, of course, another side to this argument. If Barnsley cannot turn their fortunes around and ultimately suffer relegation to League Two, Cleary's market value could fall considerably for clubs like Derby and Sheffield United, particularly with only 12 months remaining on his contract next summer.

From a financial perspective, January might just be their best opportunity to secure a substantial fee. However, selling him could also be a grave sporting mistake, too. Cleary is arguably Barnsley's most talented player and possesses the individual quality to help drag them away from the relegation places.

How likely is Reyes Cleary to remain at Barnsley after January? Comment below!

Losing that sort of match-winner midway through a difficult campaign could make an already challenging situation considerably worse. The Tykes have to weigh up the financial benefits of cashing in against the very real possibility that they need Cleary more than ever to preserve their League One status.

There are only three players with more than three goal involvements for Barnsley in all competitions this season, including Vimal Yoganathan with five, as well as both Cameron McGeehan and Cleary with nine apiece. No one can better Cleary's three assists, either.

#2141. New interest in QPR forward emerges with Arsenal among growing admirers

QPR boss Julien Stephan has shown no hesitation in offering first-team opportunities to upcoming starlets in his time at Loftus Road. Among those to break into the senior squad this season has been Leon Scarlett, and reports recently stated the 17-year-old has interest from AC Milan and Eintracht Frankfurt.

Now, it is separately claimed by Football Insider that Scarlett has interest on UK shores too. They state that the Hoops prospect is also on the radar of Premier League sides Arsenal and Brighton & Hove Albion, as well as Scottish giants Rangers.

It is said that the R’s remain confident they can keep Scarlett, given the first-team opportunities on offer at Loftus Road. However, the teenage winger did start his academy career in the Arsenal setup before switching to West London in 2025, so the chance to return and push for a breakthrough could be tempting.

Aged just 17, Scarlett has become a part of Stephan’s first-team squad. His performances in the youth setup have caught the eye, leading to his quick ascent to the senior group in West London.

Attacking injuries and the previous unavailability of Rayan Kolli brought chances for Scarlett – perhaps a little quicker than expected. Nonetheless, he has earned his five Championship appearances, starting in the 2-2 draw against Preston North End, alongside four substitute outings.

Scarlett is very much in the developmental years of his career. His early involvement at Championship level does not mean he is a fixed member of Stephan’s squad yet, especially with previously sidelined players like Kolli and Ilias Chair back in the fold, while Rumarn Burrell nears his comeback.

Interest emphasises belief in Scarlett’s potential and highlights the good work going on in the Hoops’ academy ranks. However, they must remain patient with the teenager’s progression as he continues to grow on and off the pitch.

Interest from clubs of the stature of Arsenal and AC Milan could prove tempting for Scarlett. Rangers would offer a unique and exciting opportunity too, while Brighton & Hove Albion and Eintracht Frankfurt are esteemed developers of young prospects.

All of those teams would be able to offer a high level of academy coaching and access to elite facilities. That is where they may have an edge over QPR.

However, the Hoops are more likely to be able to offer senior minutes and a quicker ascent to regular first-team selection. It means a potential Scarlett move could come down to his personal preference – does he want top coaching in elite academy facilities? Or does he want to progress with more senior exposure?

#2140. Bradford City's prospective new owners could face a £10m stadium complication

There are rumours that Bradford City could be up for sale, but any prospective new buyers of the club should be warned that any purchase of the club could come with one significant complication.

For several seasons, it looked rather as though Bradford City were living in some sort of footballing purgatory. Trapped in League Two and watched by huge crowds for that division despite barely challenging for promotion, the club seemed stuck in a loop which was proving extremely difficult to escape from.

At the end of the 2024-25 season, though, that run came to an end. Bradford were promoted back to League One following an absence of six years, and they marked their first season back by finishing in fourth place and securing a play-off place, where they lost 2-0 over two legs to Bolton Wanderers.

That form has broadly followed them into League One this season. The departure of manager Graham Alexander to Preston North End at the start of October was a big loss, and their form has dipped since then. Prior to that, though, the Bantams soared to the top of League One, again demonstrating that this is a club with untapped potential, and this in turn has reportedly attracted the interest of investors from abroad.

Recent reports from journalist Alan Nixon have confirmed that there is interest in buying Bradford City from a consortium of Thai investors who are seeking a route into the English game, with Nixon writing that, "Bradford could be for sale at the right price and the Thais, who have an intermediary with inside knowledge, are considering an official offer."

Stefan Rupp has been the public face of the club's ownership since 2016, and Rupp has been clear that he will only sell to the right buyer. Speaking to the Bradford Telegraph & Argus after the end of the 2025-26 season, Rupp said, "I'm the keeper of the keys and I would never hand it over to somebody where I have the impression he can't do any better."

How would you redevelop Valley Parade further to match Bradford City's growing ambitions? Let us know in the comments below!

On the surface, there are plenty of good reasons to have an interest in buying Bradford City. The days of them being underachievers in League Two already feel like a rapidly fading memory, and even though they've had a dip in form since the departure of Alexander, they remain one of the promotion contenders in League One again this season.

Crowds remain extremely healthy, at just shy of 20,000, and Valley Parade is a stadium which hosted Premier League football for two seasons at the turn of the century, representing a city of half a million people in which they remain the only professional football club.

But when The Athletic's Matt Slater reported on the possibility of the Bantams being sold in March 2026, he mentioned a potential sale price of around £10 million. This seems very low for an upwardly mobile League One club, but there's a very good reason for this modest valuation, and it comes down to what the club doesn't own.

The Bantams have been on the rise over the last couple of seasons, and they're now being linked with a takeover as they push for a Championship place.

In an era during which a Championship striker is having a £70 million price tag slapped on his head to deter predators, spending £10 million to buy an entire football club sounds like a bargain, but with the sale of any football club it can be beneficial to pay attention to the detail of what's actually being bought or sold.

And in the case of Bradford City, the club owns neither Valley Parade nor their training ground. Valley Parade belongs to former chairman Gordon Gibb's family pension fund. Bradford reportedly pay more than £500,000 annually to lease the ground, with the existing agreement due for renewal in 2028. Bradford have been giving consideration to possibly leaving the ground over the cost of the rent for at least a decade and a half.

And in the lower reaches of the EFL, the pure real estate value of the ground can be the biggest single cost aspect of any potential takeover. Without that, any new owner is essentially buying the player and staff contracts along with a license to operate the club in the EFL, and Bradford City, like the overwhelming majority of clubs, are not a profitable enterprise, with the last available set of company accounts showing a £3 million loss on a revenue of £8.6 million.

Earlier this year, the Bantams Supporters Trust entered into a consultation period with fans over what to do about the stadium, with a valuation of it put at between £8 million and £10 million. It's not known whether there are covenants limiting its use. But regardless of this, the Trust confirmed that they believe that the club's intention is to seek to continue to lease the stadium when that renewal comes up.

It would be advantageous to any new owners to take ownership of the ground itself. To do so would save them more than they're paying their highest-earning player every year, while its value would increase as land prices increase, and it would allow the club to redevelop the site more easily if they were looking to increase their revenues another way round.

But throwing the ground into the sale mix could double that original £10 million valuation, and although that would still represent stunning value for money were the club able to fight their way back to the Premier League, sale prices can vary wildly even within League One.

When Rob Couhig bought Reading from Dai Yongge in May 2025, for example, it was reported that the sale price was £30 million, but this included the Select Car Leasing Stadium and the club's training ground. But when Leicester City were put up for sale in September 2026, it was for a staggering £224 million, though this did include state-of-the-art training facilities, land around the stadium for redevelopment, and Leicester's Belgian sister club, OH Leuven.

Buying a football club at any level of the game can be a gamble, and the recent IFR report into the state of football finances stated that two-thirds of club have liabilities which exceed their assets and rely on owner funding to survive - making them technically insolvent - while only seven of the 116 professional clubs in the top five men’s divisions did not record a loss for the last available financial year.

All of this means that, while £10 million to buy a football may sound like a bargain, it's almost certain that this price is reflective of the extreme likelihood being that the cost to any new owner wouldn't end at that point. In the case of Bradford City, it wouldn't include the stadium itself, and this would likely double that price, while the new owner would also be on the hook for covering the club's losses, which at the last available count amounted to £3 million for one year.

Even at a sale price of £20 million to £30 million, buying Bradford City could prove to be a bargain. But so lop-sided are the finances of the majority of English football clubs that they may have to get the club all the way to the Premier League to turn a serious profit on their investment.

Speculation involving the former Liverpool coach is nothing new to supporters in this part of South Wales in recent weeks, but one positive has emerged should Matos decide to move on to a third managerial role within the space of 12 months.

Matos was appointed by Swansea on November 24th of last year, three weeks after the Welsh outfit parted company with Alan Sheehan.

Jürgen Klopp's former Anfield assistant was lured from Maritimo through a reported £880,000 compensation fee, and subsequently transformed the Swans' form for the remainder of the season as they finished 11th - nine points outside the play-offs whilst boasting Golden Boot winner, Zan Vipotnik.

Ranking the Championship clubs based on how successful they've been since the start of 2001/02

"This is something very special, it is a feeling myself and the players love, and the desire to keep building that and sharing great moments together is one of the key motivations in extending my contract with the club."

#2137. How Dave Challinor honed his craft with Celtic, Brentford trips before taking Sheffield Wednesday job

Out-of-work managers often take the time out of the spotlight to evolve their coaching game, and this was no different for new Sheffield Wednesday boss Dave Challinor. Between leaving Stockport County and taking the SWFC job, the 51-year-old has revealed Celtic’s Martin O’Neill and Brentford boss Keith Andrews’ influences.

In an interview with The Star, Challinor revealed he had dinner with O’Neill, alongside watching his Celtic side train and play. On O’Neill, the Owls boss said: “He is a really inspirational character. He is a figurehead for that football club. The success he’s had at such a massive place is enormous.”

Challinor then toured Brentford’s training ground and took inspiration from how the Bees prepare for set pieces.

“Brentford, I suppose for any club in England, or across the world, really, just the way they go about it and the way they’ve done it from the very, very top, is unbelievable,” he said. “They are an inspiration not just to me as a manager, but I’d suggest football clubs the length and breadth of the country.”

Challinor has plenty of experience in the dugout, but for someone who has followed his trajectory, there is always more to learn. He has journeyed through the football pyramid from Colwyn Bay to Sheffield Wednesday in 16 years, so he can take valuable lessons from those who have pedigree at a higher level.

In terms of his managerial abilities as an individual, Challinor will have taken plenty from O’Neill at Celtic. The 74-year-old progressed through lower leagues like the Sheffield Wednesday boss before managing big clubs in top leagues, such as Leicester City, Celtic and Aston Villa.

The sheer amount of knowledge and experience O’Neill has gained over the years means he will have had some valuable nuggets of advice to pass down to Challinor.

As for Brentford, touring their training ground would have been more of a lesson in full club management, and what can help traditionally ‘smaller’ clubs compete with the elite. The Bees are not the wealthiest club by any means, but they are sustainably run from the top to the bottom, and meticulous training sessions and match preparation means they are now in their sixth season as a Premier League side.

Challinor will have taken valuable lessons from these trips, and it will only benefit SWFC.

David Storch has expressed that Sheffield Wednesday are just at the start of a long-term rebuild. Challinor was not appointed with immediate success in mind, he was brought in to lead a new era at Hillsborough.

Some of the Hillsborough faithful will expect a strong push for promotion from League One this season, and justifiably so. Despite Storch stating the need for patience, the squad is strong enough to emerge as a strong contender at the top end of an evenly matched third tier.

Challinor’s willingness to learn and take inspiration from other managers and model clubs is an excellent attribute. Despite the success he has found in the dugout already, his desire to grow as a boss is only a good thing for SWFC as they rebuild under his leadership.

#2135. Ruben Neves drops new 'crazy' Wolves claim

Former Wolves midfielder Rúben Neves has revealed he is looking forward to playing at the Cardiff City Stadium again, as he reflected on a ‘crazy’ 1-0 win over the Bluebirds earlier in his career.

The 29-year-old is currently playing for Al-Hilal, with the move to Saudi Arabia coming after six years at Wolves. Neves had a remarkable time at Molineux, initially joining the club from Porto when they were in the Championship.

Despite the risk, it was a move that worked out, as he starred for Nuno Espírito Santo’s side as they won the title in the second tier, before recording consecutive 7th-placed finishes in the Premier League.

Neves would ultimately make over 250 appearances in Old Gold, and his quality, leadership and loyalty means he is regarded as a modern-day legend at the club.

During that period, there were many high points for Wolves and Neves, and one of the standout moments came during the 2017/18 campaign, when Wanderers won the Championship title.

In April of that season, Nuno’s side made the trip to take on fellow promotion rivals Cardiff, and the two teams played out one of the most remarkable games in Championship history.

Neves had given the visitors the lead with a trademark free-kick, but Cardiff missed the chance to equalise in stoppage time when Gary Madine saw his effort saved by John Ruddy. Incredibly, the Bluebirds would be awarded another spot-kick, but Junior Hoilett hit the bar with the last kick of the game.

That prompted wild celebrations, with Cardiff boss Neil Warnock famously snubbing Nuno’s offer of a handshake after the game.

So, it’s a stadium that will hold many memories for all Wolves fans, and Neves is back in the Welsh capital with Portugal, as Jorge Jesus’ side prepare to take on the Dragons in the Nations League.

Inevitably, Neves was questioned about coming back to the Cardiff City Stadium, and he told BBC Wales that it remains one of the ‘craziest’ games he has ever been involved in.

“I remember (it) very well! It was a crazy match, one of the craziest matches in my life, and fortunately we won that match.

“I scored a free-kick, and then in the last minutes they had two penalties, they missed both penalties, and of course, after that there was a bit of confusion on the pitch because the emotions were high.

“But it was a fantastic match, and it’s a stadium that brings me good memories.”

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

This is nice for Wolves fans to hear, as they still love Neves, and it will be great for them to see that he still looks back on his time at the club fondly, with all supporters hoping that he might one day return for a second spell at some point before he retires.

Obviously, the Cardiff game is one that will live long in the memory, and it’s no exaggeration to say that it was an ending to a match that you rarely ever see.

It was a huge disappointment for Cardiff at the time, but, to their credit, the Bluebirds did manage to put the defeat behind them, as they went up automatically with Wolves - and they did actually beat them at home in the top flight.

What is the craziest match you have seen? Let us know in the comments below.

But, that night was pivotal for Wolves, and, even after everything Neves went on to achieve at the club, and in his career, it was clearly a game that stands out.

So, he will have fond memories of Cardiff, and Neves will hope to leave with another win with Portugal against Wales on Tuesday night.

#2134. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2133. Swindon Town tipped to take Ian Holloway action after Grimsby Town taunts

Swindon Town have been tipped to potentially part ways with experienced manager, Ian Holloway, after the Robins were defeated heavily by his former employers, Grimsby Town, at the County Ground on Saturday afternoon.

The Robins are currently 16th in League Two having taken 19 points from their first 15 matches, but have won just twice in their last 14 encounters across all competitions.

Saturday's 4-1 loss against the high-flying Mariners was also Swindon's third on the spin, having been eliminated from the FA Cup against League One side, MK Dons, as well as losing in the EFL Trophy to Plymouth Argyle.

Supporters in Wiltshire have grown increasingly frustrated with the club's recent situation, as the club have continued to stagnate in the fourth tier in the five years of Clem Morfuni's ownership.

Holloway recently surpassed two years in charge at the County Ground, but after finishing 12th in his first full season last term, there are concerns as to whether his team can show any signs of progress based on their recent form.

Holloway is, arguably, the EFL's most experienced manager at present, having taken charge at matches across all levels for 30 years, including Premier League spells with Blackpool and Crystal Palace.

The 63-year-old has gained a strong reputation for various incidents over time, and this season, that has been no exception for the Swindon boss.

After slamming Manchester City for their alleged financial misconduct, Holloway claimed last month he was a "Dead. Man. Walking," within a viral interview clip, which he was reminded about by the supporters of his former club as David Artell's side ran riot, with Aaron Drinan's goal for the hosts a mere consolation after strikes from Andy Cook, Josh Andrews and a brace from Iwan Morgan.

Hollowaytook charge of Grimsby between December 2019 and 2020, winning 13 of his 39 matches in charge, accumulating a 33 percent win ratio in the process.

The experienced figure resigned at Blundell Park following a 2-1 loss to Bradford City which had the club lying 20th in League Two at the time, and the Mariners were eventually relegated back to the National League months later, albeit for a solitary season.

As such, the North Lincolnshire fanbase are not particularly fond of Holloway, who, in return, criticised their actions in his post-match interview.

“On that performance, it is what we deserved, and that hurts me bitterly, because that was a club that I used to work for," he said.

“You wouldn’t think so, would you? The use of language [from their fans] was disgusting, in my opinion, but that is what happens in modern-day football.

“They didn’t say a word when we beat them 4-0 up there two years ago, did they? It is what it is," he added.

Many notable teams have been emotionally exhausting to support in League Two, but which club tops our list at this moment in time?

Discussing the action in League One and League Two from across the weekend, EFL pundits, Tom Garratt and Callum Fowler, were amongst those debating whether Holloway's future in the Swindon job is currently jeopardised.

"Another man who should be under pressure, Ian Holloway. Swindon 1-4 Grimsby," said Garratt on the Pyramid podcast.

"He's just a 'dead man walking', that's what the Grimsby fans were singing to him," Fowler immediately responded.

"Swindon's four-game unbeaten run ends at the hands of Grimsby who have won their last three, scoring 13 goals in that time."

Later discussing Holloway's immediate future, Garratt asked: "is he under pressure?" to which Fowler added: "100%. He's a nutter."

"I'm amazed he's still got a job. Get him gone," the former then stated.

#2132. A captain’s performance and unlucky to concede three – Maignan and Rabiot’s rating vs. Italy

#2131. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

As the hunt for a new boss begins, FLW has taken a look at five potential candidates who could realistically assume control at Ewood Park next.

Former Irish forward Damien Duff began his coaching career with Shamrock Rovers in his native country before working as an assistant coach for both Celtic and the Republic of Ireland men's national team.

He took his first steps as a head coach with Shelbourne in 2021, spending three and a half years in charge of the club and guiding them to a first league title in 18 years in 2024.

#2129. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2126. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

#2124. Swansea City face Vitor Matos exit twist - 4 managers must be lined up ASAP

Swansea City fans were rocked by the news on Monday night that Vitor Matos was being looked at by Ligue 1 club Strasbourg, with pressure mounting on current manager Hugo Oliveira.

The Portuguese boss was recently linked with the Crystal Palace post, but after Swansea's form dipped with four defeats in five, and the Eagles appointed Thomas Frank to replace Pierre Sage, Swans supporters felt they'd be able to appreciate Matos a little longer.

Strasbourg's official approach to Matos indicates that the drop in form in the league hasn't impacted his standing amongst managerial circles, and the Ligue 1 side, who are sitting 13th with just three wins from 10, are keen on bringing him in if they pull the trigger on Oliveira.

It's not an ideal scenario for any club to be in, but after a strong start to the Championship campaign, with the South Wales side sitting sixth after 15 games, Swansea will need to act fast to bring someone new in if Matos decides to leave.

These four coaches should be in line to take the reins.

With Swansea's recruitment drive shifting to more of a project-based outcome in the summer, following their partnership with Tony Bloom-led Jamestown Analytics, there could be a more project-heavy hire in the dugout if Matos departs.

However, if the Swans want to keep the momentum from the first 15 games high and hire someone set to continue this promotion push immediately, Carlos Corberan should be right at the top of their list.

The Spaniard has been out of work since leaving Valencia in September. He's helped his boyhood club improve after slow starts and secure a mid-table finish in 2024/25 and 2025/26, and in the Championship, he forged a reputation as one of the better coaches in the division with both Huddersfield Town and West Brom.

Corberan took both of those to the play-offs, despite them being rather worse for wear beforehand, and at the Hawthorns, especially, had his sides playing some of the best football in the division, per the underlying numbers.

There's still plenty of room to improve in this Swansea squad, despite their position, and Corberan could be the missing piece to do so. Plus, the 43-year-old could finally become a Premier League boss in South Wales, something which has eluded him so far in his career.

Perhaps not the most popular choice among the four, but Steve Cooper was the last manager to oversee something close to a successful period at Swansea, finishing in the top six in consecutive seasons between 2019 and 2021.

Since then, the Swans haven't finished any higher than 10th. No parachute payments have helped, but perhaps the numerous changes in managers haven't either.

A look at the 25 biggest clubs in England according to AI chatbot ChatGPT.

Swansea fans weren't totally enamoured with the football being played; others were totally fine with it. What's important, though, is that it brought about results at this level, and for a Swans side who want to maintain this play-off finish, bringing in someone who hasn't finished below the top six at this level wouldn't be a bad decision.

Cooper knows how to come in midway through a campaign and oversee results, too, having taken Nottingham Forest from the bottom of the division to play-off success in the space of eight months.

The task isn't as drastic back in South Wales, so he should be able to achieve some success.

In Matos, Swansea found success with a young, up-and-coming coach from a foreign league. Perhaps they could do so again, but this time with a name they know all too well from his time as a player in South Wales.

What Pablo Hernandez is doing in his first managerial role with boyhood club Castellón in the Spanish second tier is massively going under the radar.

He took over as interim boss in September last year, oversaw their best finish in nearly 20 years, and now has them second with nine wins in 13 games. They've conceded just five goals, which is quite remarkable, and they could be on their way to La Liga for the first time since the 1990/91 campaign.

The 41-year-old already has that connection with Swansea, spending two seasons there as a player and helping them win the EFL Cup in 2013, so he knows what it's like for the South Wales side to be in the top flight and competing regularly.

It may be difficult to persuade him to leave this project at his boyhood club, but the possibility of helping bring a team back to the Premier League could be tempting enough.

Another project-based manager could see Jack Wilshere make the step-up into the Championship after a year cutting his teeth in League One with Luton Town.

The 34-year-old was unable to help the Hatters earn promotion in his first half-season in charge, but his side did show signs of excellence in the latter stages once things started to properly click. Luton went 12 games unbeaten to end the year, winning the EFL Trophy along the way.

Who do you think should replace Vitor Matos if he leaves for Strasbourg, Swans fans? Let us know in the comments.

Now, this season, Wilshere has his side playing good football; the underlying numbers have them among the best teams in the division, and they currently sit fourth, five points off Stockport County in second and with a game in hand.

Swansea haven't shied away from hiring out of the third tier. They did so with Russell Martin in the summer of 2021, and that came after he'd overseen mid-table finishes at MK Dons.

Wilshere is in a much better position as a manager now, and is one of the more promising young coaches in the EFL. He has Championship potential, and Swansea could be the side to harness that.

#2123. GdS: A midfielder with a handbrake – Sylla’s journey to USG and why Milan are interested

AC Milan have been considering a move for Massire Sylla recently and the Senegalese defender’s stock is only rising currently.

There are plenty of defensive options for Milan in the winter mercato. Goncalo Inacio is probably the preference for most, but the profile of Sylla cannot be ignored. Another signing for the future, he’s similar in many ways to Sankhoun Diawara, and the Frenchman has already started to have an impact.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Gazzetta dello Sport have looked a little at his background and his profile. A physical defender, he has previously admitted that he’s a lover of ‘the fight’; a player who loves duels and wins them a lot of the time.

Called up to the Senegalese national team this month, his story is one of patience, tenacity and the ability to persist when faced with obstacles. His career started at Diambars Academy, one of the most notorious in Senegal, starting as a midfielder but eventually moving back to defence.

His coaches wanted him to learn to play with his feet, but his defensive qualities shone, hence the move back. With that, though, he started to see the ball more and both aspects of his game improved substantially.

From there, he joined Pau in 2024 and within six months, he was playing for the senior team in Ligue 2. At 19, he moved to Lyn and then Union Saint-Gilloise paid €2 million for him last January.

It wasn’t an immediate fit, though, and he was called overexcited, thanks to his yellow card record. Yet, he’s settled and now is a key figure in a three-man defence and picking out his best traits are difficult because there are so many.

#2120. Don Goodman delivers brutal Bolton Wanderers warning

Bolton Wanderers have had a rollercoaster first third of the campaign upon their return to the Championship, and though the Trotters have impressed at the top end of the pitch, a lot has been left to desire at the back.

Bolton, following their return to the Championship for the first time since 2019, endured an incredibly busy summer transfer window orchestrated by head coach Steven Schumacher, with as many as 13 new arrivals heading to the Toughsheet Community Stadium.

Among those was an almost entirely new forward line, as the likes of Jayden Nelson, Ryan Hardie, Thelonius Bair, Kylianne Dong, and Samuel Iling-Junior joined the club.

Bolton have hit 20 goals in 15 league matches so far - a steady record. However, with 29 shipped in the other direction, improvements must be made at the back, as an EFL expert has shared their thoughts on the situation.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

Former West Bromwich Albion, Sunderland, and Wolverhampton Wanderers striker Don Goodman has delved into punditry since his retirement from professional football, and has garnered a reputation as an expert on everything in the EFL.

The 60-year-old is not afraid to say exactly what he thinks about the ongoing situations at clubs across the 72, and has shared his thoughts and concerns right now at Bolton, saying: "If you go to watch Bolton Wanderers, you know you're in for a terrific match.

They have been all season. They're a great watch.

"I suspect Steven Schumacher wants them to be entertaining, but here's the issue. They are conceding way too many goals. That will prove costly over a 46-game season.

"They've conceded 29 goals in 15 games. That's almost two goals a game. It means you have to score three goals to win a match. That might happen occasionally, like when they won 4–3 away at Norwich, but it's rare you can rely on scoring three every week.

"They have to become harder to score against. If they don't, I fear they could end up straight back in League One. They're great fun to watch, but they must tighten up defensively."

Only the bottom three of Preston North End, Burnley, and Derby County have shipped more goals than Bolton at this stage of the season, as it has become crystal clear exactly where Steven Schumacher's side needs to address if they are to be successful this year.

Despite this, only Stoke City, Sheffield United, Birmingham City and Swansea City have kept more clean sheets than the Trotters' four after 15 games, as when Bolton concede, it tends to come in waves.

They have conceded three or more goals in seven games this season, as Schumacher simply must find a way to keep the scoreline to a minimum if his side is to pick up enough points for survival come the end of the campaign.

A seven-point buffer separates them from the relegation zone right now, but it could take just a couple of weeks for that to change, as Bolton cannot afford to continue to ship goals at the rate they have been.

What have you made of Bolton Wanderers this season? Is Don Goodman's assessment of the Trotters correct? Share your thoughts below!

Schumacher has worked his way up the league to establish himself as one of the best young coaches in the EFL, having earned promotion to the Championship with Plymouth Argyle and Bolton, whilst keeping Stoke afloat in the second tier against the odds.

Now, he must try and establish the Trotters back in the second tier after seven years away, and whilst on track to do just that as things stand, if the defensive record doesn't improve sooner rather than later, he runs the risk of slipping down the table.

Goodman's warning must be taken into account by Schumacher, who should use the recent international break as an opportunity to do just that.

#2117. Official: RedBird launch ‘Milan Sports & Entertainment’ company – the initial focuses

Today, RedBird Capital Partners have announced the creation of a new company that will focus specifically on AC Milan.

This season, Gerry Cardinale’s commitments to Milan have been seen much more. The American has been at every game in Serie A, proving his desire to be closer to the team and it feels like his relationship with the club (and fans) is growing deeper.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

He’s still got room to learn, but it’s a substantial improvement from recent years and now, he’s taken a new step forward.

As MilanNews relays, RedBird Capital Partners have now launched a new holding company for anything and everything related to the Rossoneri, calling ‘Milan Sports & Entertainment’, MSE for short.

This company will ‘consolidate RedBird’s Milan-based investments in sports, infrastructure, and entertainment’ and organise individual assets into a ‘multi-sport platform’.

The report continues, stating that MSE will help to further transform Milano into one of the ‘most influential sports and entertainment markets in the world’ by combining its importance in fashion, design, culture, sports and entertainment.

Above all, though, it’s another representation of RedBird’s long-term investment and commitment to the club (and the city).

San Donato is also referenced, with MSE making a commitment to the area, and there are plans to create a stadium and arena management business, which could eventually culminate in the Rossoneri’s NBA team.

Finally, RedBird have formed a new leadership team for the group, with Brett Yormark appointed the CEO of the company. He will report directly to Cardinale and will lead the ‘development and implementation of MSE’s strategy, overseeing current portfolio businesses and evaluating future investment opportunities.’

#2115. EFL star facing jail risk for major on-pitch incident and it's caused a stir

A York City star could be facing the threat of potential jail time for a shock incident that actually occurred on the pitch - and it's causing quite the reaction as a controversial legal battle begins to gather pace.

York finally ended their ten-year exile from the EFL in emphatic fashion at the end of the previous campaign by finishing at the summit of the National League table on 108 points and just two clear of second-placed Rochdale, who would resort to gaining promotion through the play-offs following a dramatic final-day clash for the ages between the two sides.

With the league title on the line for the victors, the encounter played out on a knife-edge at the Crown Oil Arena and 95 minutes of football were played before the deadline was broken when Emmanuel Dierseruvwe appeared to have won the contest for the hosts when he struck past York's Harrison Male.

The Ministermen only needed a point from proceedings, though, and capped off one of English football's most pulsating final-day showdowns after Josh Stones buried an all-important equaliser in the last kick of the game to shatter Rochdale hearts and take the fifth-tier title back to Yorkshire instead.

However, a major flashpoint emerged prior to Stones' leveller in the wake of Dieseruvwe's opening strike, which led Rochdale supporters to believe that they had won the affair in the very closing stages.

Dale fans promptly stormed the pitch in celebration, and in all the commotion, one of York's promotion heroes in Hiram Boateng appeared to have been pushed to the ground before retaliating in a manner that now leaves the midfielder shrouded in legal trouble.

Widely-shared social media footage at the time showed Boateng on the floor in the wake of Rochdale's goal, with the 30-year-old having seemingly been pushed over and subsequently goaded by celebrating supporters - and pinned down on the floor, too, it has been claimed.

The scenes were unsavory to say the least, as Rochdale fans invaded the pitch to taunt and antagonise York players despite the game still having numerous minutes of additional time to be played out.

As stewards acted to break up the fracas, Boateng responded by punching a home fan, who promptly fell to the floor himself.

It was quickly disclosed afterwards that the FA would be investigating the matter, and proceedings have now been taken up a notch.

That's because a fresh report published by The Sun on Monday evening has revealed that Boateng, who has made just four appearances for Scott Lindsey's side in League Two so far this season, could face jail after being charged with a criminal offence.

The investigation has been taken to court, where Boateng has been charged with using threatening, abusive or insulting behavior with intent to cause fear of or provoke violence.

It's said that the Crystal Palace academy product's case was heard with no pleas on Monday morning at Manchester magistrates' court, with the Crown Prosecution Service (CPS) confirming the charges brought against the player in question.

A Home Office study has revealed which fanbases have seen the most arrests in English football, with those of some EFL clubs ranking high

Whether Boateng will actually spend time behind bars remains to be seen, of course, but the legal battle remains at large and it's caused quite the reaction from the many backers he has in his corner at York.

The former Exeter City and MK Dons star has retained the full backing of everybody at the LNER Community Stadium since the altercation, and club chairman Matt Uggla is among those to have spoken out in support of the popular midfielder.

Boateng, having joined York in the summer of 2025 following his departure from League One outfit Mansfield Town, played a leading role in the Ministermen's successful title charge during his debut campaign and has barely kicked a ball in anger this time around, with injuries limiting the well-travelled midfielder to just two fourth-tier starts.

Ironically, however, York faced Rochdale for the first time since the promotion-defining battle back in April, and with the fixture having been carried out in Greater Manchester, Lindsey's side would come out on top when Ollie Pearce scored the only goal of the game after 56 minutes, and Boateng himself was in the starting lineup.

Now, with the investigation against Boateng beginning to intensify following fresh developments, Uggla has responded to show his support and dismay at the charges being brought against the City starlet.

Speaking via his X account yesterday evening, Uggla wrote: "A fan runs onto the pitch to deliberately antagonise a player, yet it's Hiram who ends up facing a criminal charge.

"Where's the accountability for the person who invaded the pitch and provoked the situation? Absolutely ridiculous.

Uggla, of course, is far from the only figurehead to reserve strong backing for Boateng, who many believe was firmly within his right to retaliate after Rochdale supporters had committed a criminal offense in the first place by invading the field of play to antagonise opposition players.

Echoing the school of thought of many, this one fan called for the case to simply be thrown out of court in argument of injustice towards Boateng.

Another referred to the situation as "scandalous", citing the inarguable fact that one person had the right to be on the pitch and the other simply did not.

Indeed, the case has prompted cries for an enhanced systemic approach towards player safety and welfare in which greater protection and prevention from such events is provided, with several fans asking why the Dale supporter has not been identified and publicised for their own offences.

An Oldham Athletic supporter, meanwhile, has called for the fan to feel the same legal repercussions in an impassioned rant.

Whether or not Boateng was right for hitting out a fan has been hotly-debated online - and will be in the courts, of course - but this punter feels as though the midfield man did nothing wrong in the first place and the pitch-invader received just what they deserved in an act of "self defence" from York's promotion-winner.

However, not everybody has spoken in defence and support of Boateng.

It's clearly the general consensus, though others have argued that Boateng did not need to retailiate in such a fashion.

What do you make of the case? Was Hiram Boateng in the right here and should the York City midfielder's actions go unpunished, or does he deserve the legal charges? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below.

Rather interestingly, one York fan hopped over the other side of the fence and, in a claim that has not gone down too well among the City faithful, explained how Boateng's punch was unnecessary in spite of the wrongs of the aggravating supporter.

Whereas some Rochdale fans have slammed the behaviour of one of their own, others have taken a less-sympathetic stance towards Boateng and believe that his actions - regardless of whether the supporter was right to be on the pitch or not - cannot be allowed to go unpunished.

It's a controversial old case, and the direction of its travel will only be determined in due course.

For now, at least, all York supporters and power-brokers alike will intend on doing is continuing to defend the well-liked midfielder against the charges, which the overwhelming majority believe to be unjustified in light of what led to his actions.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

United have several confirmed absentees ahead of the trip to Italy. Patrick Dorgu, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are ruled out, while Leny Yoro is suspended for the European fixture after picking a red against Atletico Madrid.

Lisandro Martinez is a major concern after coming off at half-time against Leeds with a thigh problem. Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are all doubtful for the game.

Como will be without Assane Diao, who is sidelined with a thigh injury. Moise Kean has a shin problem and is a doubt, while Trevoh Chalobah is also a fitness concern with a muscular issue.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

UK viewers can watch the Champions League fixture live on TNT Sports 2 and HBO Max.

#2112. Rio Ferdinand disappointed Man Utd didn’t sign midfielder better than Elliot Anderson

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Rio Ferdinand has admitted he is disappointed that Manchester United failed to sign Ayyoub Bouaddi, with the highly-rated midfielder now making an impression at Manchester City.

Manchester City beat several rivals to secure Bouaddi’s signature over the summer in a deal worth £86 million. The Morocco international became the Premier League’s most expensive teenager, and Rio Ferdinand believes Manchester United missed out on a special talent.

The former United defender was particularly impressed by Bouaddi’s second-half cameo during City’s Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain last week. His performance reminded Rio Ferdinand why he had been talking about the youngster throughout the previous year and during the summer transfer window.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Ferdinand highlighted how Bouaddi was the highlight of the game against PSG and who they missed.

“He came on and showed PSG that they may have missed out on someone who was on their doorstep when he was playing in France,” Ferdinand said.

The former England international also confirmed that Manchester United had been interested in bringing the midfielder to Old Trafford, but the transfer fee was too expensive.

“Manchester United were in for him, 100%, but the fee was just too much,” he added.

Ferdinand was left frustrated by what he saw from the teenager, who turned 19 this month.

“Just that little cameo against PSG makes you go, ‘oh, I can’t believe we missed out on that. I can’t believe it.’ I’m so gutted, so gutted. He’s bad! He’s so calm under pressure.”

Rio Ferdinand suggested Bouaddi’s quality could soon make the midfield position in a packed Manchester City his regular role.

City already have Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez among their midfield options, but Ferdinand believes Bouaddi has the ability to stand out alongside them.

“They’ve got Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez in that midfield as well but this kid could be the best of the three, easily.”

#2111. Michael Carrick handed lifeline ahead of Chelsea trip

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

#2110. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.
